FOREWORD

Dear Student,

Rwanda Basic Education Board (REB) is honored to present the Mathematics book for Senior
Two students. This book will serve as a guide to competence-based teaching and learning to
ensure consistency and coherence in the learning of the Mathematics content.

The Rwandan educational philosophy is to ensure that you achieve full potential at every level
of education which will prepare you to be well integrated in society and exploit employment
opportunities.

The government of Rwanda emphasizes the importance of aligning teaching and learning
materials with the syllabus to facilitate your learning process. Many factors influence what you
learn, how well you learn and the competences you acquire. Those factors include the relevance
of the specific content, the quality of teachers' pedagogical approaches, the assessment strategies,
and the instructional materials available.

In this book, we paid special attention to the activities that facilitate the learning process in
which you can develop your ideas and make new discoveries during concrete activities carried
out individually or with peers.

In competence-based curriculum, learning is considered as a process of active building and
developing knowledge and meanings by the learner where concepts are mainly introduced by
an activity, situation or scenario that helps the learner to construct knowledge, develop skills
and acquire positive attitudes and values.

For efficiency use of this textbook, your role is to:

 ท Work on given activities which lead to the development of skills;

 ท Share relevant information with other learners through presentations, discussions, group
    work and other active learning techniques such as role play, case studies, investigation and
    research in the library, on internet or outside;

 ท Participate and take responsibility for your own learning; and

 ท Draw conclusions based on the findings from the learning activities.

To facilitate you in doing activities, the content of this book is self-explanatory so that you can
easily use it yourself, acquire and assess your competences.

Even though the book has some worked examples, you will succeed on the application activities
depending on your ways of reading, questioning, thinking, and grappling ideas of calculus not by
searching for similar looking worked out examples.

I wish to sincerely express my appreciation to the people who contributed towards the editing of
this book, particularly REB staffs and teachers for their technical support.

Any comment or contribution would be welcome to the improvement of this textbook for the
next edition.

Dr. MBARUSHIMANA Nelson
Director General, REB
ACKNOWLEDGEMENT

I wish to express my appreciation to the people who played a major role in the
development and the editing of Mathematics book for Senior Two students. It
would not have been successful without active participation of different education
stakeholders.

I owe gratitude to Curriculum Officers and teachers whose efforts during the editing
exercise of this book were very much valuable.

Finally, my word of gratitude goes to the Rwanda Basic Education Board staffs who
were involved in the whole process of in-house textbook production.

Joan MURUNGI,
Head of Department of Curriculum, Teaching and Learning Resources

very large numbers or very small numbers.     We can write the same in short form as
For example 1 500 000 and 0.00 001 251.       32 = 2 ื 2 ื 2 ื 2 ื 2 = 25
Writing these numbers repeatedly is
tedious and in most cases can lead to                      5 factors
errors of omission of zeros or other digits.  81 = 3 ื 3 ื 3 ื 3 = 34
To avoid this, the numbers are therefore
written in index form or in standard form.              4 factors
In this unit, we will be writing numbers      The raised numeral is called an index (plural
in index notation and in standard form.       indices), power or exponent. Representing
                                              a number in this short form is known as
                                              index notation.
                                              25 = 32 is read as 2 power 5 or 2 index 5
                                              is equal to 32.
                                              34 = 81 is read as 3 power 4 or 3 index 4
                                              is equal to 81.
                                               In general, if n = ax, then ax is the index
                                               notation of n where a is the base and
                                               x is the index.

      notation, we move the decimal place to
      the left up to the position of the number  1. On a stiff paper such as manilla

      then count the number of steps we have           sides 1 unit each. (You may use a

      as a power of 10.

  = 6x ญ y + 3z ญ 2x ญ 5y + 4z               removing the next outer bracket.

  = 6x ญ 2x ญ y ญ 5y + 3z + 4z                Example 2.8

  = 4x ญ 6y + 7z
                                              Solution
Note: In (b) above, there is no sign          {3y ญ(x ญ 2y)} ญ {5x ญ (y + 3x)}
before the first bracket so a positive sign
is assumed.                                        = {3y ญ x + 2y} ญ {5x ญ y ญ 3x}
In the expression 9 ื (7 + 3), the bracket         = {5y ญ x} ญ {2x ญ y}
means that 7 and 3 are to be added                 = 5y ญ x ญ 2x + y
together, and the result multiplied by 9.          = 6y ญ 3x
Thus, 9 ื (7 + 3) = 9 ื 10 = 90.
But 9 ื 7 + 9 ื 3 = 63 + 27 = 90.
 9 ื (7 + 3) = 9 ื 7 + 9 ื 3, which
means that 7 and 3 may each be multiplied
by 9 and the products added together.
The multiplication sign is usually omitted,
so that 9(7 + 3) means exactly the same
as 9 ื (7 + 3), just as a ื b = ab.

                                            A vector is any quantity that has
Unit outline                                both magnitude and direction. Two
                                            examples of vectors are force and velocity.
ท Concept of a vector, definition and       Both force and velocity are in a particular
     properties                             direction. The magnitude of the force
                                            indicates the strength of the force. For
ท Vectors in a cartesian plane              velocity, the speed is the magnitude.
                                            Other examples include displacement,
ท Operations on vectors                     acceleration.

ท Magnitude of vectors as its length.       Note that magnitude and direction are
                                            the two properties of a vector.

explanation of the method including an     constant is referred to as a working mean
example.                                   or an assumed mean.

Similarly, when a coin is tossed once,       Two dice are thrown together. Find the
the result will either be a head or a tail.  probability of getting a sum:
If a head occurs, a tail cannot occur.       (a) Of 8 or 3. (b) Of at least 6.
Such events in which occurrence of one       (c) Greater than 9.
excludes the occurrence of the other are     (d) Of 3 or less.
called mutually exclusive events.            (e) Not more than 3.
If A and B are two mutually exclusive        (f) That is even.
events, then P (A or B) = P (A) + P (B).
This is the addition law of probability.

getting a head or a tail in the first toss    Outcomes are {HH, HT,TH, and TT}

in the second toss. Such events in which      The probability of getting two tails is
two or more events can take place at the
same time or one after the other are called   P (T and T) = P (T) ื P (T)

                                              events on the same branch. However,
                                              the combined events of one branch is
                                              mutually exclusive to the combined
                                              event any other branch. Hence we can

Unit Summary                                                                                  Statistics and probability

1. Probability: It is the likelihood of a      Unit 11 Test
     particular event happening.
                                               1. In a group of 50 students 40 study
2. Tree diagram: It is a standard                   mathematics, 32 study physics and
     diagram used to determine all the              each student studies atleast one of
     possible outcomes in a sequence.               the subjects
                                                    a) Use the Venn diagram to

Mathematics Senior 2 Student's Book comprehensively and exhaustively
covers the Mathematics competenceญbased curriculum.
This book is appropriate as it:

      Involves the learner in the teaching and learning process through
      numerous but relevant activities;
      Uses a simple known-to-unknown approach to teach various concepts;
      Lays emphasis on competences that stimulate the thinking of the
      learner;
      Suggests practical activities that demystify abstract information;
      Tackles cross-cutting issues in an interesting and learnerญfriendly way;
      Has illustrations and photos to make learning realistic;
      Provides End-of-Unit Revision Exercises.

Accompanying this Student's Book is a comprehensive Teacher's Guide with
competence-based teaching guidelines. This will be helpful to a teacher in
applying appropriate techniques to achieve the intended outcomes.
